the API returns `1` EUPL and `99` `NOASSERTION`/none, and the payloads are EUPL-1.1/1.2 — read byte-for-byte on `8` at p714, each one byte-identical to a payload this base had ALREADY classified on its own shelves. **A `NOASSERTION` count is therefore not a count of ungranted repositories**, and the cheap remedy is sha256 identity: a payload byte-identical to one already classified needs no re-reading. 🔴 **`714-B` refutes the tempting conclusion from that.** *“The frozen EMEA column is a licence-regime artefact”* does not survive source-side measurement across `18` owners: EMEA is heterogeneous (`DFE-Digital` `79%` permissive, `ILIAS-eLearning` `0%`), and **the most copyleft owner in the entire sample is `openedx` at `66%` GPL — North America, not Europe.** 🔵 **And this table cannot be used to test the question at all: `274/274` rows are permissive BY THE ADMISSION RULE, so reading a region's licence mix off this shelf is selection on the dependent variable.** 🔴 **`714-C`: a new grant-defect class — a SPLIT grant (`LICENSE-DOCS`, CC BY 4.0) in a repository whose product IS the documentation**, found on `1` of `14` probed filings. 🔴 **`714-D`: this shelf records ONE Apache-2.0 payload under TWO byte counts and TWO hashes — `11,357`/`c71d239df917` on `3` rows and `11,356`/`43070e2d4e53` on `3` more — and they are the SAME FILE.** The cause is now identified: a probe that reads a payload through a shell `$( )` substitution silently strips the trailing newline. **So a hash mismatch between two rows is not evidence of a different grant, and every cell filed at p714 states `raw file bytes` to say which convention it used.** 🔴 **`714-F`: p714 reproduced `p704`'s exact defect in its own first draft** — fifteen rows written with the repository as a bare `owner/repo` slug instead of a link, which made the subject-counter read the first link in the *description* instead. > 🔴 **p703's first finding is that the singleton-heading rule, stated at `p699` and restated at `p702`, HELD on this shelf and FAILED ON BOTH SIBLING SHELVES IN THE SAME PASS.** `p702` fixed this page's count and published the rule. A rule enforced on the page where the defect was last seen is not enforced — all three shelves are counted and re-published in this one edit, and that is the only form of the rule that works.**
> 🟢 **p703 filed `14` rows here, `21` on foundations and `9` on verticals — `44` in all — and the eight mandated legs contributed ZERO of them.** That is the **31st consecutive nil** on the mandated search legs for this shelf. The nil is measured, not assumed: the four global legs plus the four regional legs returned `13` repository addresses in total and **`13 / 13` were already held by this base** (full list in [`trending.md`](trending.md) `703-A`). Every one of the `44` rows came from two channels the legs cannot reach: a `20`-head GitHub `topic:`/keyword sweep run **under this base's own star floor**, and owner expansion.
> 🔴 **And the sharpest result of the pass is an omission in THIS BASE'S OWN LEDGER, not in the supply.** Owner expansion on six owners returned `530` sibling repositories. Two of those owners were already represented here by exactly one row each — [`malkreide/global-education-mcp`](https://github.com/malkreide/global-education-mcp) and [`michael-borck/study-buddy`](https://github.com/michael-borck/study-buddy) — and **neither owner had ever been queried**: `75` and `243` repositories sitting one API call away from a row this base already held. `9` of p703's `44` rows came from those two owners alone, including the pass's most-adopted artefact at `55★`. **A held row is an unexplored owner, and this base has `5,257` of them.** See [`trending.md`](trending.md) `703-B`.
> 🔵 **An instrument correction, found by reading p703's own verification output: the licence-payload `sha256₁₂` this shelf prints in every cell is an OWNER fingerprint, not a repository fingerprint.** Four `malkreide` repositories filed this pass all carry `ac1fc40e61a6` at `1,069 B`, and `michael-borck`'s `talk-buddy`, `netsim` and `assessment-bench` all carry `b4fdbc9f39ce` at `1,070 B` — because an MIT payload differs only in its copyright line, which is constant across one owner's portfolio. **A dedupe or provenance scheme keyed on grant-payload hash would therefore collapse an owner's entire estate into one subject.** The hash remains worth printing as an integrity check on *re-reads of the same repository*; it is worthless as an identity across repositories, and this page had not said so.
>

### 🟢 `713-G` — the finding: the mandated regional legs return PRODUCT NAMES, and this base was only ever reading them for repository ADDRESSES

`703-A` measured the mandated legs at a 31st consecutive nil, `704-A` at a 32nd, and both concluded the four regional legs are a **market** instrument rather than a **supply** one — worth running for demand intelligence, worth nothing for repositories. **p713 makes that 33 consecutive nils on addresses and refutes the conclusion drawn from them.**

The APAC leg (`AI education APAC 2026 adoption regulation players`) returned, in prose: *"Singapore is betting on frameworks and toolkits, like AI Verify, that companies can use to test and show accountability."* **No URL, no owner, no slug — a product name in a sentence about regulation.** One `user:aiverify-foundation` query later:

|---|---|--:|---|
| [`aiverify-foundation/aiverify`](https://github.com/aiverify-foundation/aiverify) | 🟢 Apache-2.0 · `LICENSE` · `11,350 B` · sha256₁₂ `63a6b58f3c9a` · holder *"Copyright 2023 AI Verify Foundation"* | `106` | `verticals/solutions.md` |
| [`aiverify-foundation/moonshot-cicd`](https://github.com/aiverify-foundation/moonshot-cicd) | 🟢 Apache-2.0 · `LICENSE` · `11,357 B` · sha256₁₂ `c71d239df917` | `15` | this shelf |

A leg that names a *regulator's toolkit*, a *national platform* or a *ministry programme* has produced a lead that costs exactly one `user:` or `q=` query to resolve — and the eleven passes that scored those legs `0 / N addresses` were scoring the wrong unit. **The procedure change: every regional leg's output is now mined for NAMED INSTRUMENTS, and each name is resolved against the search index before the leg is scored nil.**

🔴 **What this does NOT refute.** The four **global supply legs** nil'd again, address for address, and this time the extraction excuse does not apply — they returned repository addresses and every one was already held:

### 🔴 `713-D` — the supply channel that works is one API door wide, and three of the four doors are shut in this session

Recorded exactly, so no later pass re-buys it:

| endpoint | result |
|---|---|
| `gh api orgs/{owner}/repos` | 🔴 `"This GitHub API path is not available: sessions are bound to their configured repositories"` |
| `gh api users/{owner}/repos` | 🔴 same refusal |
| `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` | 🔴 `"GitHub access to this repository is not enabled for this session"` |
| `gh api -X GET search/repositories -f q='user:{owner}'` | 🟢 **works, paginates, and carries `license.spdx_id` + `stargazers_count` + `pushed_at` + `archived` + `fork`** |
| `curl -sI https://github.com/{slug}` | 🔴 `403` for live AND nonexistent slugs — unchanged from `710-B`, re-tested at p713 |
| `git ls-remote --symref https://github.com/{slug} HEAD` | 🟢 the arbiter for liveness and for the real default branch |
| `raw.githubusercontent.com/{slug}/{branch}/{grantfile}` | 🟢 the arbiter for the grant — **subject to `713-B`** |

🟢 **Owner expansion is therefore the whole supply programme, and its yield this pass is stated exactly: `52` owners asked, `45` returned at least one permissive repository, `836` distinct permissive non-archived non-fork repositories enumerated, `763` of them NOT already held, `25` filed after relevance screening and byte-level verification — a filing rate of `3.3%` against unheld supply.** The two owners that paid best:

| owner | rows held before | repositories enumerated | filed at p713 |
|---|--:|--:|--:|
| `HKUDS` — the lab that produced this base's `DeepTutor` row | `1` | `50` | **`7`** (`4` agents, `3` foundations) |
| `instructure` — the vendor of the LMS this base's largest cohort targets | `1` | `71` | **`5`** (all foundations) |

🔴 **Neither owner had ever been enumerated, and both had been represented here by a single row for hundreds of passes.** That is `703-B`'s finding repeating with larger numbers: **a held row is an unexplored owner.** `763` unheld repositories remain from this pass alone, so the channel is **nowhere near exhausted** — the constraint is relevance screening, not discovery.

### 🔴 `713-B` — the grant probe's filename list is case-sensitive and omits the `COPYING` family, and it produced three false *no-grant* verdicts in one pass

`681-I` recorded that a licence probe run against `/main/` by habit returns `404` on a `master`-default repository and **reads as no grant**. p713 hit the same failure on a different axis — the **filename** — three times in `28` subjects:

| subject | probe said | the grant actually is | ★ |
|---|---|---|--:|
| [`moodlehq/moodleapp`](https://github.com/moodlehq/moodleapp) | 🔴 no grant | 🟢 **Apache-2.0** in `COPYING.txt` (`11,358 B`, sha256₁₂ `cfc7749b96f6`) | `1,005` |
| [`LearningLocker/xapi-service`](https://github.com/LearningLocker/xapi-service) | 🔴 no grant | 🟢 **MIT** in lowercase `license` (`1,070 B`, sha256₁₂ `99a909b026f0`) | `42` |
| [`LearningLocker/xapi-validation`](https://github.com/LearningLocker/xapi-validation) | 🔴 no grant | 🟢 **MIT** in lowercase `license` (same payload) | `12` |

🔴 **A `1,005★` official mobile client of the sector's largest platform was one filename away from being refused.** The failure mode is the dangerous one: it does not error, it returns a *clean negative*, and a clean negative is indistinguishable from a genuinely unlicensed repository — which is grounds for refusal under this base's own filing gate.

🟢 **The fix, and it is already applied to every row filed this pass:** probe the full list, in this order, at the **real** default branch from `git ls-remote --symref` — `LICENSE`, `LICENSE.txt`, `LICENSE.md`, `LICENCE`, `LICENCE.txt`, **`license`**, **`license.txt`**, `LICENSE-MIT`, **`COPYING`**, **`COPYING.txt`**, `COPYING.md`. 🔵 **And the generalisation worth more than the list:** `681-I` was branch, `713-B` is filename-case-and-family, and both are the same defect class — **a negative result from a probe whose address was constructed by convention is evidence about the CONVENTION, not about the repository.** Of the `28` subjects whose grants were read byte-for-byte this pass, **`7` default to `master`** and **`3` keep the grant outside every `LICENSE*` path** — the two sets overlap on both `LearningLocker` rows, so **a probe hard-coded to `main` + `LICENSE` would have mis-read `8 / 28`.**

### 🔵 `713-E` — a grant payload repeated byte-for-byte across owners is a cross-check, not a coincidence

Four byte-identical groups appeared among the `30`, and each one buys something:

| sha256₁₂ | bytes | subjects | what it settles |
|---|--:|---|---|
| `7eb48830c022` | `1,071` | `edulinq/autograder-py`, `quiz-composer`, `lms-toolkit` — **and this base's EXISTING `edulinq/autograder-server` row** | 🟢 the org's grant is uniform, and the earlier row's reading is **independently corroborated** a pass later |
| `68c9fc511bb1` | `1,088` | `HKUDS/RAG-Anything`, `VideoAgent`, `LightReasoner` | 🟢 one lab, one grant, three artefacts — holder *"✨Data Intelligence Lab@HKU✨"* |
| `cfc7749b96f6` | `11,358` | `moodlehq/moodleapp` (`COPYING.txt`), `aboutcode-org/scancode.io` (`LICENSE`) | 🟢 stock unmodified Apache-2.0 at **two different paths** — the same bytes under two filenames, which is `713-B` and `713-E` meeting |
| `c71d239df917` | `11,357` | `aiverify-foundation/moonshot-cicd`, `code-dot-org/javabuilder` | 🟢 stock Apache-2.0, **no copyright line in the payload** — the holder is in project metadata, not the grant |

🟢 **Operationally: a hash that matches a payload this base has already read and classified needs no re-classification, only a liveness check.** `12` of the `28` payloads read with full byte metadata this pass fell into one of these five groups, which is where the verification budget went instead.

### 🟢 `713-F` — two region placements decided by the artefact, in OPPOSITE directions, and `712-B`'s trap runs both ways

`712-B` recorded that a university acronym in a repository name is a continent-level trap (`mcp-blackboard-ucsc` is in Italy, not California). p713 hit the mirror image:

- 🟢 [`EduNLP/nemo-multistage-classroom-diarization`](https://github.com/EduNLP/nemo-multistage-classroom-diarization) — `EduNLP` reads as a generic education-NLP org with **no continent attached**. The grant payload names **"Stanford EduNLP Lab"**. Filed `North America` **on the bytes**.
- 🔴 [`ai-shifu/markdown-flow-agent-py`](https://github.com/ai-shifu/markdown-flow-agent-py) — `ai-shifu` reads `APAC` immediately; *shifu* (师傅) is Chinese for master or teacher. **The artefact ships no committed locale catalogue, no `zh` README and an English-only `pyproject.toml`.** `712-A`'s channel returns **nothing**, so the row is filed **`Global`** — an explicit placement nil, not a guess.

🔵 **The rule this settles: a name is a hypothesis in BOTH directions.** `712-A` established that the artefact declares its region; `713-F` adds that when the artefact declares **nothing**, `Global` is the honest cell and the five-value vocabulary is not at fault — which is the same answer `711-C` got wrong from the other side.

## 🟢 Sixteen agent rows added at p712 — and the pass's product is a REGION-PLACEMENT CHANNEL that reads the artifact instead of the prose, which refutes `711-C` and breaks a five-pass freeze

🔵 **The eight mandated search legs returned their 39th consecutive nil on supply at p712**, measured address by address in [`trending.md`](./trending.md) `712-G`. Every repository the four global legs surfaced — `THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC`, `HKUDS/DeepTutor`, `patchy631/ai-engineering-hub`, `microsoft/generative-ai-for-beginners`, `rasbt/LLMs-from-scratch`, `microsoft/ai-agents-for-beginners` — was **already on this shelf**, and the four regional legs returned market commentary with no repository in it at all. All `16` rows below came from `gh api search/repositories` with the grant in the query, the channel `711-A` established.

### 🟢 `712-A` — the finding: a repository DECLARES its own region in machine-readable form, and this base had been reading prose instead

`711-C` concluded that the five-value region vocabulary "CANNOT EXPRESS *Spanish-language, country unknown*", and recorded `North America 22` frozen for a fifth consecutive pass as the consequence. **Both halves of that are wrong, and the counter-example is `JhostinAleck/brightspace-mcp`.** It ships `src/shared-kernel/output/i18n/catalogs/es-419.json`. `es-419` is the UN M.49 code for Latin America — it is *precisely* the identifier for "Spanish-language, country unknown", it is committed into the repository as a translation catalogue rather than claimed in a README, and it maps onto the closed vocabulary with no inference at all: **`es-419` → LATAM**.

Four channels were used this pass, in descending order of strength. Each is a server-side or byte-level read, never a name and never a guess:

| # | Channel | How it is read | Rows placed | What it cannot do |
|--:|---|---|--:|---|
| 1 | **Shipped locale catalogue / IANA timezone** | `gh api -X GET search/code -f q='es-419 repo:…'`, confirmed against the file list | 2 | Only exists in internationalised projects. Distinguish shipped catalogues from schema *options* — see below |
| 2 | **Grant holder line** | `curl raw.githubusercontent.com/…/LICENSE`, `grep -i copyright` | 1 | Only places a row when the holder is an institution, not a person |
| 3 | **Named deployment or preset inside the artifact** | README read from bytes | 6 | Names the *served* institution, which need not be the owner's country |
| 4 | **Owner's declared profile location** | `gh api -X GET search/users -f q='user:A user:B … location:"X"'` | 7 of 27 probed | `search/users` does **not** return a `location` field, so it only answers a yes/no against a guessed place name |

🔧 **Channel 1 has a trap, and the trap is the discriminator.** `JhostinAleck/brightspace-mcp` matches `pt-BR` (14 files) and `fr-CA` (13 files) as well as `es-419` (23) — but those two appear only in the config *schema*'s list of permitted values, and the repository ships catalogue files for `en-US` and `es-419` only. **Place a row on the catalogues it ships, never on the locales it permits**, or every internationalised project lands in five regions at once.

🔧 **Channel 4 is the weakest of the four and it is the one the obvious approach reaches for first.** `gh api users/{login}` is refused outright in this session (`403`, *"sessions are bound to their configured repositories"*), and `search/users` returns `20` fields per item with `location` **not among them**. The only route is the `location:` qualifier, which is a yes/no probe against a place name you must supply. 🟢 **The useful discovery is that it batches**: multiple `user:` qualifiers OR together, so `27` logins × one call per place name resolved the whole shortlist in `~45` calls instead of `~1,200`. `26` of the `27` logins resolved in the index; `praisethefacts` did not (`712-C`).

🔴 **And the honest measurement of channel 4 is that it fails four times out of five.** `7 / 27` owners declare a parseable place; `20 / 27` declare nothing a probe can match, across `45` place names covering all five regions. **So `711-C`'s complaint was aimed at the wrong target.** The gap is not the region vocabulary's expressiveness — it is that this base had been placing rows from the owner's profile, which four fifths of owners leave empty, instead of from the artifact, which declares its region in a locale catalogue, a copyright line or a deployment list.

### 🟢 `712-A2` — the consequence, measured: `North America 22 → 28` after five passes of no movement

| Region | p711 | p712 | Δ | placed by which channel |
|---|--:|--:|--:|---|
| **North America** | 22 | **29** | **+7** | preset list (`d2l-cli`: Kennesaw State, Georgia State) · deployment note (`d2l-scraper`: UVic) · author line (`Brightspace-API`: "ECE @ Purdue") · profile (`cyanprot`: Vancouver · `YouLearn-AI`: United States) · grant holder **and** profile agreeing (`wildcat-code`: Tufts CEEO, Boston) · **plus one row UPGRADED, not added** — see `712-A3` |
| EMEA | 24 | 27 | +3 | deployment list (`blackboard-mcp`: five UK universities) · payload domain (`mcp-blackboard-ucsc`: `unicatt.it`) · institution (`uu-brightspace-cli`: Utrecht) |
| LATAM | 21 | 22 | +1 | shipped locale catalogue (`es-419.json`) |
| APAC | 62 | 63 | +1 | profile **and** artifact timezone agreeing (`Asia/Hong_Kong`, 37 files) |
| Global | 107 | 111 | +4 | `+5` filed as unplaceable, `−1` upgraded out |
| **total** | **236** | **252** | **+16** | |

🔵 **`Global 111` is still the largest column, and that is now a measurement rather than a shrug:** five rows declare no locale catalogue, no institution and no owner location, so `Global` is what the evidence supports for them.

### 🟢 `712-B` — an institutional ACRONYM is a region trap, and this one spans two continents

[`NiccoloSalvini/mcp-blackboard-ucsc`](https://github.com/NiccoloSalvini/mcp-blackboard-ucsc) reads as UC Santa Cruz to any English-speaking pipeline, which would have filed it `North America`. It is **Università Cattolica del Sacro Cuore**, in Italy. The evidence is in the payload and nowhere else: `Cattolica` ×3, `unicatt.it` ×3, `Verbalizzazione`, `Inoltro alla firma` and an Italian `SVE` exam-registration template, in a `20,932 B` README read from `raw.githubusercontent.com`. **The repository name never says Italy and the README never says Italy either** — the country comes from the institution's own domain. 🔵 The generalisation for every shelf in this base: **a university acronym in a repository name is a collision, not an address.** Resolve it from an email or web domain in the payload before it reaches the `Region` cell.

### 🔴 `712-C` — the two GitHub search indexes disagree with each other, which is a sharper form of `688-B`

`688-B` and `704-C` recorded that `search/repositories` returns `total_count: 0` for repositories that are demonstrably live. p712 observed the next layer down: **the repository index and the code index disagree about the same repository**, and the user index disagrees about its owner.

| Subject | `search/repositories` | `search/code` | `search/users` | `git ls-remote` | page read from bytes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NiccoloSalvini/mcp-blackboard-ucsc` | 🟢 resolves, `MIT`, `0★` | 🔴 **`total_count: 0` for `Cattolica`, `Verbalizzazione`, `unicatt.it` and `unicatt`** | 🟢 owner resolves | 🟢 `main` | 🟢 `20,932 B`, every term present |
| `praisethefacts/google-classroom-mcp` | 🟢 resolves, `MIT`, `0★` | — | 🔴 **owner absent from a 27-login OR query that returned the other 26** | 🟢 `main` | 🟢 `5,581 B` |
| `cyanprot/brightspace-mcp` | 🟢 resolves, `MIT`, `3★` | 🔴 absent from a 4-repo OR query that returned the other 3 | 🟢 owner resolves, `Vancouver` | 🟢 `main` | 🟢 `11,005 B` |

🔵 **The operational rule, which is stronger than "retry the search":** a nil from any GitHub *index* is evidence about the index. Only `git ls-remote --symref` and a byte read of the payload are evidence about the repository. This pass would have mis-placed one row and dropped two if a code-search or user-search nil had been read as absence.

### 🔴 `712-D` — an MIT grant whose copyright holder is *"Anonymous Authors"* has no identifiable licensor

[`Veteran-Zero/MERIT`](https://github.com/Veteran-Zero/MERIT) (filed on [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md) this pass) carries a clean `1,074 B` MIT payload whose holder line reads **`Copyright (c) 2026 Anonymous Authors`** — double-blind review anonymity leaking into the grant. 🔵 **MIT's permission is granted *by* the copyright holder, so a holder that is not a legal person is a permission no acquirer can trace.** It is not a licence defect that a `spdx_id` detector can see — GitHub reports `MIT` — and it is readable with one `grep -i copyright` on the payload this base already downloads for every row. **A new cheap check for the verification loop: a holder line that names no person or institution is a flag, not a formality.** Three further grants read this pass name a person who is not the namespace owner or a pseudonym (`PTFacts`, `Wildfell Hall`, `UniKT Team`); those are ordinary, and the distinction is identifiability, not formality.

### 🔵 `712-E` — the Brightspace/D2L field, mapped once so the search is not re-spent

This shelf carried **two** Brightspace rows before this pass — `RohanMuppa/brightspace-mcp-server` and `AnonymousOnyx22/brightspace-d2l-mcp` — against `14` Canvas-family rows. One query with the grant in it — `brightspace d2l license:mit pushed:>2026-06-01` — returned `total_count: 51`, of which `12` are substantive and `8` are filed above. **The field's shape is the finding:** every client authenticates through the user's own browser session (Playwright profile, pasted cookie, Valence token or OS keychain) because **D2L does not issue student API keys**, so the whole field converges on session reuse and none of it needs a tenant admin. Writes are rare and gated twice where they exist. 🔴 **Declared gap:** no Brightspace row is instructor-side — grading, gradebook and roster writes exist only on the Blackboard and Canvas families — so a D2L teacher-facing engagement starts from the student clients plus `RohanMuppa/Brightspace-API`'s read-only client, not from a working grader.

🔵 **And `mattdig/brightspace-scheduler`** (filed on [`../verticals/solutions.md`](../verticals/solutions.md), `5★`, MIT, `2022` holder line) **is the only artifact in the field that extends the tenant rather than reading it**: static files uploaded to Brightspace's "Public Files" area plus two Custom Links in the navbar, self-enrolled groups and the calendar doing the work. It contains no AI. It is filed because it is the attachment point an AI feature would use, and because it shows what "customising Brightspace" means when there is no plugin API.

### 🟢 `712-A3` — the channel pays for itself twice: one row was UPGRADED rather than added, from evidence found in a DIFFERENT repository

[`RohanMuppa/brightspace-mcp-server`](https://github.com/RohanMuppa/brightspace-mcp-server) has sat on this shelf since `p657` with `Region = Global`, because nothing in that repository names a country. The library filed beside it this pass — `RohanMuppa/Brightspace-API`, same owner, shared configuration and shared sign-in session — opens with **"By Rohan Muppa, ECE @ Purdue"**. 🟢 **One owner's two repositories are one placement**, so the older row is corrected to `North America` in the same pass that files the newer one. Its `Stars` cell is refreshed `62 → 64` at the same time.

🔵 **The generalisable rule: when a row cannot be placed, the evidence may be in a SIBLING repository rather than a better query.** Owner expansion was already this base's best supply channel (`703`, `709`); it is also a placement channel.

| Row | Cell as published | Re-read at p712 | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|

### 🔴 `708-D` — the star channel: all THREE doors were shut, so the cells are empty rather than wrong

`p704` recorded *“two of three doors are shut, and the third has a hole in it.”* 🔴 **At p708 the third is shut too, and it was probed, not assumed:**

| channel | probe | result |
|---|---|---|
| `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` | `gh api repos/HKUDS/DeepTutor` | 🔴 **`403`** — *“GitHub access to this repository is not enabled for this session”* |
| `github.com` HTML (repo page) | `curl -sI https://github.com/HKUDS/DeepTutor` | 🔴 **`403`** |
| `img.shields.io/github/stars/...` | `curl` for both `.json` and the SVG | 🔴 **unreachable, `http_code 000`, `0` bytes** |
| `raw.githubusercontent.com` | `curl` for `LICENSE` | 🟢 **`200`** — licence payloads readable |
| `git ls-remote --symref` | against nine subjects | 🟢 **works** — liveness, default ref and HEAD sha |
| `git clone --depth 1 --bare` | against nine subjects | 🟢 **works** — commit date and tracked-path count |

🟢 **So p708 could verify existence, default branch, HEAD sha, licence payload bytes, commit date and tree size for every subject it filed, and could not obtain a single star count.** 🔴 **The mandate's own instrument is the casualty worth naming: `curl -sI` against `github.com` returns `403` here for a repository that `git ls-remote` proves ALIVE.** A pass that used the mandate's literal gate — *“Verify every URL before writing it (`curl -sI`). A 404 is not a finding.”* — would have refused all nine live subjects, and a pass that used it to re-audit would have retired `192` good rows. 🔵 **The correct instrument under this network policy is `git ls-remote --symref` for existence and `raw.githubusercontent.com` for the grant; `curl -sI` on `github.com` is uninformative in both directions.**

`p687` named a promotion leak and built `compose/code/p687-promotion-leak/` to measure it. `p704` ran the measurement across its own candidate set and the result is stark:

🔵 **The transferable instruction, and it should change how the next pass spends its budget:** this base's shelves are not short of *discovered* supply, they are short of *promoted* supply. `trending.md` is `72,000+` lines and functions as a write-only log. **Before running a discovery sweep, sweep `trending.md` against the live tables** — it is one `grep -rlF` per candidate and it yielded `12` rows here, against `0` from the eight mandated legs. The mandated legs are not the channel; the base's own history is.

### `704-A` — the mandated legs, measured address by address: the 32nd consecutive nil on supply

All eight legs were run as specified with the year **computed** (`2026`), not hardcoded. They returned **`18` distinct repository addresses between them, and `18 / 18` were already held:**

`thu-maic/openmaic` · `HKUDS/DeepTutor` · `patchy631/ai-engineering-hub` · `gego-k12/gegok12` · `openeducat/openeducat_erp` · `krayin/laravel-crm` · `apache/ofbiz-framework` · `crewAIInc/crewAI` · `microsoft/ai-agents-for-beginners` · `microsoft/generative-ai-for-beginners` · `hcengineering/platform` · `towardsai/ai-tutor-app` · `Ebimsv/AITutorAgent` · `csmediapro/moodle-mcp-server` · `G36maid/moodle-mcp` · `peancor/moodle-mcp-server` · `vishalsachdev/canvas-mcp` · `tutornew/OpenTutor`

🔴 **`18 / 18` held.** The mechanism `p703` identified is confirmed: the legs surface secondary blogs, the blogs rank by star count, and anything star-ranked was filed here long ago. 🟡 **Two legs reported their own emptiness in so many words** — *“The search didn't turn up a ranked list of MIT-licensed AI agent projects aimed at education”* and *“I didn't find a clearly new open-source AI tutor agent released in October or November 2026”*. Recorded so the `33rd` pass does not re-buy them.

🟢 **The LMS/MCP leg was the one exception and it produced exactly one row** — [`Mattlo0546/canvas-mcp`](https://github.com/Mattlo0546/canvas-mcp), filed above. That leg is narrower than the four global ones and it reached a subject with `2★`, below every blog's ranking floor. **Narrow, mechanism-named legs still work; broad "top N" legs do not.**

### 🔴 `704-C` — the star channel, named precisely: two of three doors are shut, and the third has a hole in it

`p703` recorded `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>` as `403` and left the mechanism unstated. `p704` probed all three channels and the picture is now exact:

| Channel | Result | Usable for stars? |
|---|---|---|
| `curl https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>` (HTML) | **`378`-byte JSON stub**: *“GitHub access to this repository is not enabled for this session. Use add_repo to request access.”* | 🔴 No — the page is intercepted, not fetched |
| `curl https://api.github.com/repos/<owner>/<repo>` | **`403`** | 🔴 No |
| `gh api -X GET search/repositories -f q='repo:a/b repo:c/d …'` | 🟢 **Works**, returns `stargazers_count` **and** `license.spdx_id`, batchable | 🟢 Yes — **the only star channel** |
| `raw.githubusercontent.com/<owner>/<repo>/<ref>/LICENSE` | 🟢 **Works** | — the only first-hand *grant* channel |

🔵 **The consequence is the part worth carrying:** stars for a non-attached repository are available **only** through the search index — so a repository the index omits has **no star channel at all**. `704-D` is that case. This is not a transient proxy failure and retrying will not fix it; it is the session's repository scoping, and the only lever is attaching the repository.

### 🔴 `704-D` — `Harikeshav-R/canvas-mcp` refused: the grant payload names a DIFFERENT owner, and that settles it

`p703-D` established that this base's `sha256₁₂` column **identifies the owner, not the repository**. `p704` has the clean confirming instance, and it resolves a canonical-versus-fork question entirely from the grant file:

| Subject | Grant | Bytes | sha256₁₂ | Holder line |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [`vishalsachdev/canvas-mcp`](https://github.com/vishalsachdev/canvas-mcp) — **already a shelf row**, `318★` | `LICENSE` | `1,071 B` | `5385a26e2fac` | `Copyright (c) 2025 Vishal Sachdev` |
| [`Harikeshav-R/canvas-mcp`](https://github.com/Harikeshav-R/canvas-mcp) — candidate | `LICENSE` | `1,071 B` | **`5385a26e2fac`** | **`Copyright (c) 2025 Vishal Sachdev`** |

**Byte-identical grant, and the holder it names is the other owner.** The two also publish the same description verbatim (*“up to 102 tools and 8 agent skills…”*). 🔴 **Refused as a row** — it is a copy of a subject this shelf already carries, so filing it would have created a duplicate under a second owner's address and inflated the count by one. **Fifth canonical-versus-fork instance on this shelf, and the first settled without reading a single line of code.**

🔴 **And it recurs:** `Harikeshav-R/canvas-mcp` is **absent from `gh api search/repositories`** — seven `repo:` qualifiers were submitted in one call and `total_count` came back `6`, with this one missing, while `git ls-remote --symref` resolves it to `refs/heads/main`. That is the `688-B` / `690-B` false-negative class again, now at a **third** instance. **A search miss is still not grounds to retire a row**, and it is also not grounds to doubt a repository that `ls-remote` answers for.

### 🔴 `704-G` — a NEW defect sub-class: the grant reference that dangles, at `104★`

[`DMontgomery40/mcp-canvas-lms`](https://github.com/DMontgomery40/mcp-canvas-lms) (`104★`, pushed `2026-05-31`) states in its README:

> `## 📄 License` — *“MIT License - see [LICENSE](LICENSE) file for details.”*

**The file does not exist.** Ten grant paths were probed at the real default ref (`main`), every one `404`:

`LICENSE` · `LICENCE` · `license` · `license.md` · `LICENSE.MD` · `MIT-LICENSE` · `MIT-LICENSE.txt` · `COPYING` · `COPYING.txt` · `docs/LICENSE`

`gh api search/repositories` independently reports `license.spdx_id: NONE`. 🔴 **Refused.**

🔵 **Why this is a sub-class and not just “no licence”.** This shelf already refuses projects with no grant — `samrathreddy/Tutor-multi-ai-agent` (`5★`) was refused this pass on exactly those grounds, and it makes **no** licence claim anywhere. The dangling case is worse in the one way that matters: it **advertises MIT**, with a link, to anyone who greps the README and stops there — and it does so at `104★`, which is adoption, not obscurity. **An unlicensed repository that says nothing is safe to skip; one that says “MIT” and links to nothing is a trap.** The discriminator is cheap and it is now named: resolve the link, do not read the claim.

🟢 **This shelf was re-verified structurally on an independently written script, and it is clean.** `84` data rows between the header separator and the end of the table; **`84` distinct subjects** taken as the first repository link in each row (**`0` duplicates**, `0` rows without a link); **`0`** rows whose cell count differs from the header's `6` (histogram `{6: 84}`); **`0`** `Region` cells outside the closed vocabulary. 🔴 **The audit's own first run was wrong, and the bug is published because it is a trap laid exactly where this shelf is weakest.** The initial vocabulary check normalised region cells by stripping whitespace, which turns `North America` into `NorthAmerica` — not a member of the closed vocabulary — and reported **`12` out-of-vocabulary cells that do not exist**. 🔵 **`North America` is the only one of the five legal values that contains a space, so any normaliser that strips interior whitespace will mis-flag that region and only that region.** Strip decoration (`**`, backticks, status emoji) and trim the ends; never strip interior whitespace. The `12` false positives were exactly the `12` North America rows.

🔵 **Where the pass's actual yield went.** The regional legs are recorded leg-by-leg at [`../intel/market.md`](../intel/market.md) (all four regions nil on content, each written down explicitly); the probe-failure class and the header-as-data sweep at [`./trending.md`](./trending.md) `691-B`/`691-D` and [`../intel/trends.md`](../intel/trends.md) `T102`/`T103`; and the LATAM supply measurement, which is the pass's one new fact, at `market.md` under `LATAM` with the build consequence at [`../compose/patterns.md`](../compose/patterns.md) `P71`.

🔴 **One refusal is worth more than the five additions, because three independent instruments disagreed and only the byte read was right.** `July-Tea/EssayMate` (`39★`, an essay grader — squarely inside the assessment gap this page tracks) is **CC BY-NC-SA 4.0**: *NonCommercial*, which bars exactly the commercial derivative this KB exists to scope. `gh api` reported **`NOASSERTION`**; **this base's own `ingest/probe-grant.sh` reported `CC-BY-4.0`** — collapsing the `NC` and `SA` terms and yielding a *permissive-looking* label for a licence that forbids the use case. **`672-D`: a grant classifier that normalises a licence *family* can drop the one term that decides usability, and it fails in the unsafe direction.** The `NOASSERTION` caveat `p658` attached to GitHub's classifier now extends to this repository's own.


🟢 **p679 adds four rows, and three of them are *placed* — which is the thing this base said it could not do last pass.** p678 filed nine addresses and placed **none**, and recorded that it had measured placement empty on both channels rather than assumed it. p679 ran the four mandated English legs and the four regional legs and got its supply from the **regional** legs, which is where the placement came from: `lab-grader-skill` → **EMEA** on a shipped dialect default (Palestinian colloquial Arabic), `Canvas-grading-with-AI` → **North America** on *two* independent channels (Chapman University with a matching `chapman.edu` domain, and a `FERPA Compliance` section — a US federal statute), and `AI_Tutor_Release` → **APAC** on a national curriculum publisher (人教版 / PEP). The fourth row, `bp-master-agent`, is filed **`Global` on purpose**: Brazilian Portuguese is its *subject*, not its deployment geography, and a language-of-instruction signal is weaker than any channel this shelf places on.

🔵 **Two new evidence classes enter the ledger at p679, both read from the repository's own files at the real default ref.** (1) **Shipped locale default** — a named dialect or timezone that a deployer gets without choosing it. `lab-grader-skill`'s Arabic default is one; so is `Gego-K12/gegok12`'s `.env.example`, which ships `TIMEZONE = 'Asia/Kolkata'` and thereby places that platform row **APAC** (see `verticals/solutions.md`), a row this base had carried unplaced. (2) **Regulatory regime as placement** — a repository built against a jurisdiction-specific statute is evidence about jurisdiction. FERPA places `Canvas-grading-with-AI` in North America more reliably than its author's location would.

🔴 **One refusal and one dangling reference, both filed so they are not re-searched.** `end13s/AI-Grading-Tool` would have been a **second** North America row on the strongest channel — the README states it was *"Built at Calvin University"* (Michigan) and it does exactly what this shelf wants (Moodle roster in → LLM checks each submission against the rubric → grades back out as Moodle CSV). It is **not filable**: `ls-remote` resolves (`main` @ `1453f503168e`) but there is **no grant payload** under `LICENSE`, `LICENSE.md`, `LICENSE.txt`, `COPYING` or `LICENCE`, and the repository page shows no licence either — so it is all-rights-reserved by default and Globant cannot build on it. Separately, `bncc-dev/bncc-pacotes`'s own `LICENSE` names **`bncc-dev/bncc-site`** as the canonical home of the project's trademark, but `ls-remote` on that address **fails** — the reference is dangling, i.e. the trademark boundary is declared against a repository that is not public.

🔵 **Channel map, re-measured at p679 — and the previous pass's warning was right.** `api.github.com` is **blocked** for every third-party repository in this session (`403`, *"GitHub access to this repository is not enabled for this session"*), and so is `gh api`; `github.com` HTML **via `curl`** returns nothing parseable, and `img.shields.io` returns empty. The **repository page is nevertheless readable**, through a different endpoint, and that is where every star and fork count on this page's p679 rows came from. `git ls-remote --symref` and `raw.githubusercontent.com` remain the reliable existence and grant channels. A channel being dead on one client does not make it dead.


## 🟡 One agent row added at p686 — and the pass's transferable result is a defect in a FIELD THIS BASE READS, not a new tutor
🔴 **`license.spdx_id` from the GitHub API is not a licence, and p686 measured how badly.** The eight mandated legs plus the four regional legs returned **no agent address this base did not already hold** — the **28th consecutive nil** on this shelf — so p686 spent the pass on the instrument instead, sweeping `22` GitHub topic heads (`310` candidate repositories, `4,118` already held by this KB) and then **resolving every candidate the API reported as `NOASSERTION` by reading its grant payload at the real default ref**. Of `31` such repositories: **`12` are permissively licensed and adoptable** (Apache-2.0, MIT, MIT/X, Unlicense), `1` is **ECL-2.0** (OSI-approved, Apache-derived), `4` are copyleft, `5` carry a **Creative Commons** grant that cannot license software at all, `2` ship **no licence file whatsoever**, and **`1` is outright proprietary**. `NOASSERTION` therefore spans the full range from public domain to all-rights-reserved: **the field carries no information, and both ways of acting on it are wrong.** A pipeline that drops `NOASSERTION` as "unknown" discards the only permissively-licensed institutional LMS in this KB (OpenOlat, Apache-2.0); a pipeline that waves it through adopts a repository whose LICENSE reads *"All rights reserved — Source Available — Personal Use"*. Census: `repos/trending.md` → **p686**.
🔴 **The mandated `curl -sI` URL check is independently useless here, re-confirmed at p686 with controls at both signs.** Through this environment's agent proxy it returned `HTTP/1.1 200 Connection Established` for `torvalds/linux`, for `moodle/moodle` **and for `gmilano/this-repo-does-not-exist-99887766`** — a check that cannot return `404` cannot fail, so it would have "passed" every invented slug. Every row filed at p686 is instead verified by `git ls-remote --symref` (real default branch **plus** head SHA) and a grant payload read from `raw.githubusercontent.com` (byte count **plus** `sha256₁₂`). Negative control on that pair: `ls-remote` fails to authenticate and `raw` returns a true **`404`**. The pair discriminates; `curl -sI` does not.

After `maxChallenges` steers (default `5`) the reply closes as-is — and the README is explicit that **the cap is a leak guard, not the gate; the score threshold is the gate**. 🟢 Two properties make it reusable as *assessment* infrastructure rather than just agent polish: the reviewer sees **every real user prompt** of the session (tagged `<all_user_prompts>`) with plugin-injected context — file notices, `AGENTS.md`, skill text, system reminders — **filtered out**, so it scores against what the learner actually asked; and threshold, on/off, cap and reviewer model are all re-tunable live with no host restart (config at `~/.dsh/answer-reviewer.json`, overridable via `REVIEWER_CONFIG_PATH`, plus a JSON API). Swap the generic quality rubric for a marking rubric and this is a re-gradeable formative-feedback loop |

It encodes the real rules rather than asking a model to be fair: total topic evasion, non-dissertative form and under-length each **zero** the essay, *tangenciamento* caps C2/C3, and C5 is scored by an explicit five-part checklist (agent, action, means, purpose, detail). 🟢 **It ships its own evals harness** (`backend/evals`) measuring against human-graded essays, plus network-free tests behind a fake LLM — so the grader's quality is a *measured* number, not a claim. 🟢 **BYOK and genuinely offline-capable:** OpenRouter/Gemini/Groq/OpenAI/Claude **or Ollama locally**, with **PaddleOCR on-device** for handwriting, so a public-school deployment needs no per-seat licence and no egress. 🔵 **Optional self-consistency** (sample each competency *N* times, take the median) is the cheapest variance control on these shelves. 🟡 **It states its own limits**: explicitly unaffiliated with **INEP/MEC**, and the score is labelled an estimate for study, never an official mark — the refusal path `P72` asks for, written by the project itself. A labelled span can be shown to a student, checked by a teacher and audited by a regulator; a 0–200 number produced by a language model can do none of the three. 🟢 **Runs in the browser** (no server, no key, no egress) and publishes weights on Hugging Face. 🔵 Bilingual pt-BR/English README. 🟡 The README offers “MIT / Apache-2.0”; the payload at the real default ref is **MIT** and that is what this cell certifies. The author states plainly that the framework does not guarantee grading quality, only that a bad run is visible within two minutes. Traditional Chinese first, with an English summary. |

Four constructions worth copying: **(1) root-cause over marks** — it traverses a curriculum **prerequisite skill graph** to find the missing building block (a child failing two-digit addition has usually not mastered place-value bundling), instead of reporting a score; **(2) 5-day micro-pathways** of 10–15 min/day using *physical manipulatives* (straws, pebble grids, number cards), so the intervention needs no second device; **(3) offline-resilient** by design, for schools with intermittent or absent connectivity; **(4) no public ranking**, explicitly to avoid stigmatising young learners, and correct rendering of **Gujarati/Hindi matras and conjuncts**, which most English-centric tooling clips. Next.js 16 / React 19 / TS 5.7. ## 🟢 Fourteen agent rows added at p709 — and the pass's transferable product is a ONE-LINE TEST that reconciles two of this shelf's own placement rules

**`709-A` — The mandated search legs nil for the 37th consecutive pass, and this time the nil is total.** All four global legs and all four regional legs were run for the computed current year (`2026`, derived from the clock, not hardcoded). They returned `10` repository addresses with any education bearing — `anandpatikat/guru-latihan-dinamis`, `OPTIMETA/PAIDEIA`, `GarethManning/education-agent-skills`, `rohitg00/ai-engineering-from-scratch`, `openeducat/openeducat_erp`, `TapXWorld/ChinaTextbook`, `microsoft/ai-agents-for-beginners`, `NousResearch/Hermes-Agent`, `krayin/laravel-crm`, `hcengineering/platform` — and **`10 / 10` were already held by this base.** `0` rows here came from a mandated leg. All `14` came from a GitHub `search/repositories` sweep under this base's star floor (`12` legs) plus owner expansion (`5` owners, `96` sibling repositories).

**`709-B` — The star channel `p708` declared shut is OPEN, and the useful part is *which* part of it.** `p708` recorded `gh api` → `403`, `github.com` HTML → `403` and `img.shields.io` unreachable, and left `7` rows with `—` in the `Stars` cell. At p709, measured both signs: `gh api -X GET search/repositories` returns `total_count`, `stargazers_count`, `license.spdx_id` and `pushed_at` normally, **but `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` returns `403` with *“GitHub access to this repository is not enabled for this session”*.** So the per-repository endpoint is session-scoped while **the search endpoint is not** — which means a pass that tests only `repos/…` will wrongly conclude the whole API is shut. Every star figure on this pass's `14` rows comes from the search endpoint.

**`709-C` — The test. `p669` says a named institution places a row; `p684` says an LMS integration target does NOT. Both are right, and the thing that separates them is countable: does the target exist in exactly one jurisdiction?** `u:find` is the University of Vienna's catalogue — one. `LearnUs` is Yonsei's portal — one. `JEE`/`NEET` are India's national entrance exams — one. `DAM`/`RA1` is a Spanish vocational qualification — one. **Those place.** Canvas, Gradescope, Brightspace, Moodle and **ILIAS** are products deployed in many countries — **those do not**, and `ilias-mcp` is filed `Global` at `13★` this pass to keep the rule against a German-origin temptation. Urdu is official in **two** jurisdictions, so `UrduPlanner-Agent` is `Global` while `CogAIt` is `APAC`, and the two rows sit on this shelf as the worked contrast. **The test subsumes `p670`'s results rather than competing with them:** a translated README declares an audience (many jurisdictions), and a person has no jurisdiction at all.

**`709-D` — Two placements were about to be filed off regex false positives, and both were caught by inspecting the matched text instead of the match count. This is the defect the task instruction warns about — *“inferences become wrong data”* — caught in the act, twice in one pass.**

| subject | token scanned | hits | real | false | what the false hits actually were | outcome |
|---|---|--:|--:|--:|---|---|
| [`Marcoh05P/EdOU`](https://github.com/Marcoh05P/EdOU) | `br` | **9** | **0** | **9** | the HTML line-break tag `<br/>` in architecture diagrams, plus `browser`/`bridge` nearby | 🔴 LATAM placement **refused**; row filed `Global` |
| [`balejosg/tiza`](https://github.com/balejosg/tiza) | `ESO\b` (case-insensitive) | **4** | **2** | **2** | the tails of *proceso* and *peso* | 🟡 EMEA placement **kept**, but re-grounded on the `2` standalone hits **plus** a named sibling corpus |

**The rule this yields: a locational token is evidence only after its matched text has been read.** A count is not a finding. `br` is a country code *and* an HTML tag; `ESO` is a Spanish curriculum stage *and* the last three letters of two common Spanish words. Both scans were run with `grep -oiE` — the same instrument this shelf uses everywhere — so the exposure is general, not specific to these two rows.

**`709-E` — LATAM returned zero REPOSITORIES, which is a different finding from zero novel ones, and it is written down because silence looks exactly like coverage.** Eight institution-shaped LATAM legs (`mcp server universidad siu guarani`, `sigaa mcp server`, `moodle api agente alumnos python`, `unam universidad agente IA`, `usp universidade agente IA alunos`, `tec monterrey IA agente educacion`, `enem vestibular IA agente`, `mcp server escola brasil educacao`) returned **`total_count: 0` — `0` distinct subjects, not `0` after filtering.** Four broader Spanish/Portuguese legs did return `28` subjects, of which `3` were novel and permissive, and **all three failed placement on their own evidence**: [`jessic-amendes/acelera-ai-educacao`](https://github.com/jessic-amendes/acelera-ai-educacao) is MIT and alive but scores **`0`** Brazil-only institution tokens (`INEP`, `IDEB`, `Censo Escolar`, `ENEM`, `SAEB`, `BNCC`) and its own README declares a `comparar_paises()` function grouping by World Bank `Income Group` — **it is multi-country by construction**; `patyfreitasbr/agente-educacional-ia` was last pushed `2025-05-10`; and `balejosg/tiza` placed EMEA, not LATAM. **So the LATAM gap on this shelf is a supply gap in institution-bound Spanish/Portuguese education tooling on GitHub, not a search gap — and `LATAM 19` standing still at `206` rows is the honest number.**

**`709-F` — The mandated `curl -sI` URL check was re-tested at both signs and it is still worthless in this environment, exactly as `p684` found.** `curl -sI https://github.com/gmilano/negative-control-does-not-exist-p709` returns **`HTTP/1.1 200 Connection Established`** — the proxy's `CONNECT` line, not the origin's status — so a repository that does not exist "passes". Every row filed at p709 is instead verified by `git ls-remote --symref` (real default branch **and** head SHA) plus a grant payload read from `raw.githubusercontent.com` (byte count **and** `sha256₁₂`). **The same negative control was run through that instrument and it correctly returned `DEAD_OR_UNREADABLE`**, so the instrument discriminates where the mandated one cannot. `14 / 14` rows alive, `14 / 14` MIT read from the bytes.

**`709-G` — A cross-check that fell out of the grant reads: [`balejosg/tiza`](https://github.com/balejosg/tiza) and [`balejosg/normativa-madrid`](https://github.com/balejosg/normativa-madrid) ship byte-identical grants** (`1,065 B`, sha256₁₂ `e5d43db441b9` both) — consistent with one owner, and a reminder that a payload hash identifies a *licence text*, not a repository. `p684`'s `canvas-mcp` collision was the same observation used as a warning; here it is used as a consistency check.

🟢 **What did work: a date-windowed `topic:` sweep.** `20` English topic heads restricted by `created:` returned `812` distinct repositories, **`685` novel against the `5,553` slugs this base holds (`84.4%`)** and **`309` novel *and* permissive** — because the heads were swept before but never *windowed*, so everything created since the last sweep was invisible to a head-only query. The Spanish/Portuguese heads (`T130`'s channel) returned `255` distinct, `224` novel, `65` novel and permissive; the North-American instrument heads (`topic:canvas-lms`, `topic:powerschool`, `topic:k12`, `topic:common-core`) returned `259` distinct, `209` novel, `74` novel and permissive.

A check that cannot return `404` cannot fail, so it would have passed every invented slug this pass could have written. **Every row below is instead verified by `git ls-remote --symref` (real default ref), by reading the grant payload byte-for-byte from `raw.githubusercontent.com`, and by `gh api search/repositories` for `license.spdx_id` and `stargazers_count`.**
🔴 **New instrument defect, found at p701: `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` is blocked for this session and `gh api search/repositories` is not.** The direct repository endpoint returns *"GitHub access to this repository is not enabled for this session"* for every slug outside the session's own scope, while the search endpoint answers for all of them. A pass that reads metadata from the direct endpoint will record `35` API misses and may conclude the repositories do not exist. **Batch `repo:` qualifiers through `search/repositories` instead** — `12` per call resolved `35/35` and then `17/17` here.

### 🔴 `688-B` — `gh api search/repositories` returns `total_count: 0` for a live repository, and this base has been leaning on that channel

This base records, in [`../intel/market.md`](../intel/market.md), a channel finding it has relied on for several passes: `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}` returns `403` for every repository not attached to the session, while `gh api search/repositories -f q='repo:{owner}/{repo}'` returns full metadata — *"never conclude from a `repos/` 403 that an address is dead."* **That channel has a false-negative class, measured this pass:**

| query | `total_count` |
|---|--:|
| `repo:mitodl/open-learning-ai-tutor` | 🔴 **`0`** |
| `repo:mitodl/open-learning-ai-tutor fork:true` | 🟢 **`1`** — `1★`, `MIT`, `main`, pushed **`2026-11-07T06:47:41Z`** |

**GitHub's search index excludes forks unless `fork:true` is asked for.** The repository is not merely alive; it was pushed *hours before this pass ran*. A `search/repositories` nil is therefore evidence about the index, not about the address — exactly the shape of the `repos/` `403` error this base already corrected once. 🔵 **Transferable rule: a two-arm probe. `git ls-remote --symref` answers "does it exist"; `search/repositories` answers "what does the index hold"; only the two together answer "is it dead".** `ls-remote` resolved this address on the first attempt.

### 🔴 `681-B` — `NOASSERTION` is uninformative in **both** directions, and the mandate's own wording walks into it

The standing instruction on these shelves is *"focus on MIT / Apache-2.0 / BSD"*. The cheapest way to do that is GitHub's licence facet. **p681 measured what that costs, on five repositories read the same day:**

| repo | API `spdx` | grant payload, byte-read | what the facet would have done |
|---|---|---|---|
| [`Saidkhusayn/student-dropout-early-warning`](https://github.com/Saidkhusayn/student-dropout-early-warning) | 🔴 `NOASSERTION` | 🟢 **verbatim MIT** + a correct `CC BY 4.0` data carve-out | **dropped a permissive row** |
| [`ChrisWhiteSr/teach-skill`](https://github.com/ChrisWhiteSr/teach-skill) | 🔴 `NOASSERTION` | 🟢 **MIT** + an upstream portions notice that *verifies* | **dropped a permissive row** |
| [`GarethManning/education-agent-skills`](https://github.com/GarethManning/education-agent-skills) | 🔴 `NOASSERTION` | 🔴 **CC BY-SA 4.0** (`1,230 B`, sha256₁₂ `f952182df4eb`) | correct by accident |
| [`PreTeXtBook/pretext`](https://github.com/PreTeXtBook/pretext) | 🔴 `NOASSERTION` | 🔴 **GPL-2-or-3**, and the referenced GPL text is **absent from the repository** | correct by accident |
| [`workforce-data-initiative/skills-api`](https://github.com/workforce-data-initiative/skills-api) | 🔴 `NOASSERTION` | 🔴 **non-commercial**, University of Chicago, and the grant names **a different program** | correct by accident |

🔵 **So the facet is not a weak signal — it is a signal with no direction.** Two of five `NOASSERTION` readings sit on verbatim MIT; three sit on grants that forbid what this base needs. The three the facet got right, it got right for reasons that have nothing in common with the two it got wrong. **Rule, and it is not new, only now priced: the grant is the bytes at the resolved default ref. The API licence field is metadata about a parser.**

### 🔴 `681-C` — two mandated legs returned a *permissive licence claim* that does not survive one `curl`

| the claim, as the leg stated it | reading at `2026-11-06` |
|---|---|
| `Amanatar/student-mental-health-companion` — *"the only result I can confirm is MIT-licensed"* | 🔴 `spdx = null`, and **no grant at `LICENSE`, `LICENSE.md`, `LICENSE.txt`, `LICENCE`, `COPYING`** on the resolved default ref `main`. `0★` |

🔵 **This is the class `662-G` opened and it has now paid out three times, always in the commercially dangerous direction** — permissive-when-actually-unlicensed or permissive-when-actually-copyleft. An unlicensed repository is *worse* than a copyleft one for a client deliverable, because there is no permission to analyse. Both claims were specific, confident and attached to a named repository, which is exactly the shape that reaches a slide.

### 🟡 `681-D` — the mirror problem, measured twice in one pass, and the discriminator is not the one you would reach for

Two addresses were returned by legs this pass as if they were distinct projects. Both are **the same commit as a project this base already holds or filed**:

| address returned by the leg | the real project | HEAD | grant bytes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `annagraceinfiniti/education-agent-skills` | [`GarethManning/education-agent-skills`](https://github.com/GarethManning/education-agent-skills) | **both `135d6275e7db`** | **both `1,230 B`, sha256₁₂ `f952182df4eb`, `© 2026 Gareth Manning`** |
| `johnnylibretexts-ai/pressbooks-cli` | [`johnnyrobot/pressbooks-cli`](https://github.com/johnnyrobot/pressbooks-cli) | **both `9f7d388a877a`** | **both `1,073 B`, sha256₁₂ `48c3921fa1fc`** |

🔵 **`git ls-remote HEAD` settles it in one call, and star counts do not.** Neither mirror is a fork in GitHub's sense (`fork = false` on the one that is searchable at all), so the `fork` flag is useless here; the commit identity is decisive. 🔴 **And the consequence differs by licence, which is the half worth carrying:** the `education-agent-skills` copy redistributes **CC BY-SA 4.0** content under a second account — the ShareAlike obligation travels with the copy, so cloning the mirror buys no relief from it. The `pressbooks-cli` copy is MIT and the choice is cosmetic. **The mirror is a licence question, not a tidiness question.**

### 🔴 `681-E` — refused, and named so the collision is not re-bought

[`yaniv-golan/pretext-skill`](https://github.com/yaniv-golan/pretext-skill) — **MIT** (`1,068 B`, sha256₁₂ `cd34da18ee9a`, `© 2026 Yaniv Golan`), `15★`, pushed **`2026-11-06`**, and returned by the OER leg as an AI agent skill for **PreTeXt**, the open-textbook authoring system. **It is not.** It is a skill for [`@chenglou/pretext`](https://github.com/chenglou/pretext), a TypeScript library that predicts line breaks and text heights without DOM reflow. Its own README says so in the first sentence under the badges. 🔵 **The collision is exact and it is not a spelling variant** — two unrelated projects, both named *pretext*, one in scholarly publishing and one in text layout, and the search index cannot tell them apart because neither is wrong about its own name. Any future OER leg on this base must disambiguate on the *subject matter* in the README, never on the repository name.

### `677-E` — 🔴 the autograding leg returned two projects presented as open source, and neither grant supports it

The mandated grading leg surfaced both of these as open-source autograders; **both claims fail at the payload.**

- 🔴 [`sdrangan/llmgrader`](https://github.com/sdrangan/llmgrader) — an LLM autograder for engineering courses with a Gradescope exporter, an MCP server for agent-assisted authoring, and grading traces, described by its author as in real classroom use. Its `LICENSE` is a **bespoke instrument titled “PySilicon Research License”** (© 2026 Sundeep Rangan). **It is not an OSI licence and not a licence family this base carries.** A named research licence is a *narrower* grant than any of MIT, Apache-2.0 or BSD, and research-only terms are the ones that cannot be scoped out of a commercial deliverable. **BLOCK.**
- 🔴 [`emorynlp/LLM-Grading`](https://github.com/emorynlp/LLM-Grading) — the release behind the *LLM-as-a-Grader* paper, whose abstract states that the authors *“publicly release LLM-as-a-Grader, an open-source grading toolkit, along with all code and evaluation protocols.”* The repository is live on its real default ref `master` with a `README`, **`0★`, no description, and no licence payload** under `LICENSE`, `LICENSE.md`, `LICENSE.txt`, `license.txt`, `LICENCE` or `COPYING`, on `master` or `main`. **“Open-source” in an abstract is not a grant.** Usable only after the authors add one — which is a two-line email, and is the remedy, not a refusal. **BLOCK pending a grant.**

🔵 **Why this pair is worth a section rather than a line.** Both are *good software* and both would be attractive to a client. The failure is not quality — it is that the sector's grading layer is where research code and product code overlap most, and research code is where the grant is least likely to exist. **T20/T30 say permissive autograding exists at university scale; `677-E` says the *LLM* half of it still mostly does not.** Pair the two: build on `autolab/Autolab`, `anubislms/anubis` or `webtech-network/autograder` (all permissive, all on the shelves), and treat the LLM grader as a component you write.

### 🔴 A named mechanism for `NOASSERTION` — p658's caveat, now explained rather than observed

p658 recorded that `gh api search/repositories` returned `NOASSERTION` for three repositories whose payloads read `MIT`, `AGPL-3.0` and `AGPL-3.0`, and warned that `NOASSERTION` means *the classifier did not match*, never *no licence*. p659 reproduces the effect **with the mechanism attached**, on a controlled set — five repositories, one author, one licence, one pass:

| Repo | Payload bytes | Payload read at first hand | `search/repositories` `license.spdx_id` |
|---|---|---|---|
| OPTIMETA/PAIDEIA | 1,068 | MIT | `MIT` |
| OPTIMETA/PAIDEIA-codex | 1,068 | MIT | `MIT` |
| OPTIMETA/PAIDEIA-opencode | 1,068 | MIT | `MIT` |
| OPTIMETA/PAIDEIA-mcp | 1,068 | MIT | `MIT` |
| OPTIMETA/PAIDEIA-Hermes | **1,397** | MIT + appended `Attribution` | 🔴 **`NOASSERTION`** |

🔵 **Four of five agree; the fifth disagrees, and the only variable is `+329 B` of text appended after the grant.** The classifier is matching the file as a whole against a template, so *anything appended to a stock licence file flips a correct `MIT` to `NOASSERTION`* — an attribution block, a second grant, a vendor notice. That is a **false negative generated by good practice**: `PAIDEIA-Hermes` is `NOASSERTION` *because* it documents its upstream properly. 🔴 **Consequence for this base:** an API `license` field is unsafe in *both* directions — T38 recorded it unsafe in the permissive direction, and this is the conservative direction. The byte count is the signal that distinguishes them: a payload at the stock size with a `NOASSERTION` verdict is a classifier failure to investigate; a payload off the stock size needs the diff read before either field is trusted.

### 🟡 An author key built on the copyright line would split this one author in two

Across five sibling repositories by the same person, the grant payload names the holder two different ways: `Taewoo Park` (`PAIDEIA`, `PAIDEIA-codex`, `PAIDEIA-Hermes`) and `TaewoooPark` (`PAIDEIA-opencode`, `PAIDEIA-mcp`) — three `o`s and no space. Both are the same author (`taewoopark.com`, one `OPTIMETA` namespace, ports cross-referenced in each README). 🔴 **`author-key-census` keys on the copyright string, so this specimen produces two authors from one** — recorded here as a live fixture for that instrument rather than as a defect in the repositories, which are internally consistent.

This base's own rule (`gate-group-lineage`) is that **`N` distinct HEADs does not mean `N` projects, and the citable unit is the family.** All five addresses are real, live, MIT and byte-read at p659; all five are filed in `evidence/address-ledger.tsv`. Only the canonical gets a shelf row — four more rows would inflate the count of *projects* this shelf has found by four, which is exactly the padding this KB refuses.

## 🟢 New at p656 — what this shelf still does not give you, and where it now lives

No agent row was added this pass, and none was removed. The twenty rows above are the agents; padding the count would make this page worse. What p656 changed is the layer *beneath* them, and it changes how every row here gets delivered.

🔴 **Until p656 this shelf answered "what can we build" and had no answer to the two questions that follow it in every engagement:** how does the agent appear inside the LMS the client already runs, and how does anyone prove it worked. Both are now answered on `repos/foundations.md`, with ten permissive rows promoted there this pass:

- **Getting in without forking anything.** [`tsugiproject/tsugi`](https://github.com/tsugiproject/tsugi) (Apache-2.0, 377 ★), [`dmitry-viskov/pylti1.3`](https://github.com/dmitry-viskov/pylti1.3) (MIT, 138 ★), [`jupyterhub/ltiauthenticator`](https://github.com/jupyterhub/ltiauthenticator) (BSD-3-Clause, 73 ★). An LTI 1.3 tool launches inside Moodle, Canvas, Brightspace, Open edX, Sakai and OpenOlat alike, and because it is a separate work it **inherits copyleft from none of them**. Every agent on this shelf can reach every one of those platforms through one integration.
- **Proving it worked.** [`yetanalytics/lrsql`](https://github.com/yetanalytics/lrsql) (Apache-2.0, 144 ★) with [`adlnet/xAPI-Spec`](https://github.com/adlnet/xAPI-Spec) (Apache-2.0, 955 ★). Every interaction an agent has becomes a statement — actor, verb, object, result, timestamp — in the client's own database. 🔵 **This is the deliverable that turns a pilot into a renewal**, and it is the direct answer to `intel/trends.md` **T10** and **T19**, which have recorded for many passes that the evidence base is this sector's weakest point.

🔵 **Worth stating plainly, because it was the defect this pass found:** none of those components is new to this repository. They were verified in the append-only logs passes ago — `lrsql` appears in 230 files — and had never reached a shelf. **The agents on this page have always been deliverable; this base simply had not written down how.** Wiring is specified end to end in `compose/patterns.md` **P17**.

## 🔴 The licence unit is the dependency graph, not the repository

🆕 **p670 — the strongest instance this section has yet carried, and it is a *new mechanism*: the dependency is not declared, vendored or pinned in a manifest at all. It is fetched at run time.** `DaRL-GenAI/instructional_agents_studio` is MIT in full (`1,093 B`, read this pass). Nothing in its `package.json`, its `123` tracked files or its LICENSE would fail a licence scan. Its `NOTICE`, however, states that the slides/documents editor **is not in the package** and that the app **downloads it on first use** into `~/.cache/instructional-agents/editor/<version>/` (SHA-256-checked, or overridden by `IA_EDITOR_DIR`) — a trimmed build of [`ranuts/document`](https://github.com/ranuts/document) embedding **ONLYOFFICE `sdkjs` and `web-apps`**. **Verified independently at p670, not taken from the NOTICE: `ranuts/document` is AGPL-3.0 (2,018★) and `ONLYOFFICE/sdkjs` is AGPL-3.0.** 🔴 **So the licence unit is not even the dependency *graph* here — it is the dependency graph *at run time*, which no static scan of the repository can see.** A client's SBOM, built from the manifest, would report this product as MIT. 🔴 **And there is a second exposure in the same disclosure:** when the download is absent the app falls back to a **hosted editor at `edit.chaxus.com`**, a third-party domain named in the shipped `bin/cli.mjs`. For a product whose pitch is that the API key and the data stay in the browser, **the documents themselves can leave to a third party by default** — which is the EMEA data-residency question, reopened by a fallback path. 🔵 **Transferable rule, stated so it is not re-derived:** *ask what the product downloads after installation, not only what the repository contains.* The remediation is in `compose/patterns.md` **P39** — keep the MIT shell, replace the editor.

Three of this page's rows carry a permissive badge over a tree that contains obligations the badge does not mention. All three were read at first hand at p652.

| Row | Badge | What is actually in the tree | What it means |
|---|---|---|---|
| **OpenMAIC** (42.9k★) | MIT | `packages/mathml2omml` is **LGPL-3.0-or-later** (7,652 B payload; `"license"` field verbatim in its `package.json`), declared `"workspace:*"` in the root manifest and built as the **first step** of `build:packages`. `packages/pptxgenjs` is MIT and fine. | A deliverable built from OpenMAIC **links an LGPL-3.0-or-later component**. Stated precisely, because over-reaction is the risk in both directions: LGPL-3.0 is not the GPL and does not make the whole application copyleft. It does require that the component stay *replaceable* with its source available, and "or-later" means a client cannot freeze the version of the terms. For a bundled, transpiled JS component in a shipped web app, replaceability is an engineering requirement, not a formality. Workable — but it must be designed in, and the licence badge gives no hint of it. |
| **OmniEdu** (687★) | Apache-2.0 | The payload is stock Apache-2.0 and covers the **code**. The README grants nothing for the weights: *"Model weights and third-party datasets and benchmarks retain their respective licenses."* Backbones are Qwen. | The artefact a client actually deploys — the weights — has **no named grant anywhere in the repository**. `huggingface.co` does not resolve under this session's network policy, so the cards could not be read. The toolkit and training code are unambiguously usable; the weights are an open question. |
| 🟢 **OpenOlat** (449★, `verticals/solutions.md`) | Apache-2.0 | The payload is genuine Apache-2.0 and **270 declared dependencies contain exactly one copyleft item**: the *optional* MySQL Connector/J (GPL-2.0 + Universal FOSS Exception). Verified absent: iText (AGPL-3.0) and JasperReports (LGPL), the two usual killers in a Java LMS. Hibernate is 7.4.14 and hibernate-validator 9.1.6 — Apache-2.0 eras, not the old LGPL-2.1 Hibernate. | **The discipline paying off in the other direction, which is worth recording.** Three of the rows in this table are permissive badges over obligations; this is a permissive badge that **survives** a manifest read. And the one exposure it does have is removable at zero cost: the in-tree PostgreSQL JDBC driver is BSD-2-Clause and a first-class backend, so specifying PostgreSQL makes the tree permissive end to end. A manifest read is what turns "Apache-2.0" from a claim into a scopeable position. |
| **open-moodle** (not filed, 0★) | MIT | Depends on **PyMuPDF (AGPL-3.0)** for PDF text extraction, stated in its own README. | An AGPL dependency in a network-served agent is the most consequential copyleft on this shelf. The dependency sets the obligation, not the repository. |

**The rule:** reading `LICENSE` is necessary and not sufficient. Read the manifests — `package.json`, `pyproject.toml`, `Cargo.toml` — and for a model, read the weight card. Four of the five grant-defect classes this base has catalogued would pass a licence-badge check.

### 🆕 A sixth defect class, found at p653 — and the repository carrying it has the best licence hygiene on this shelf

`wwrwbs/AI_AWE`'s `LICENSE` is **1,865 bytes**. Canonical Apache-2.0 is 11,357. A size test calls that a truncated grant; reading it shows something different and worth naming separately:

> **Class 6 — grant by reference.** The file contains the Apache-2.0 *notice* (the short boilerplate Apache itself prescribes for source headers) — `"Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 … You may obtain a copy of the License at http://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0"` — in place of the terms themselves.

**This is materially stronger than Class 5** (`frappe/education`'s 19-byte `License: GNU GPL V3`), and the distinction is the practical one: the notice form contains the operative grant language, names the exact version, and points at a canonical immutable URL, so *what you are permitted to do is unambiguous*. What is missing is the copy of the text that Apache-2.0 §4(a) requires a redistributor to supply. **Verdict: usable, with a one-line fix to ask for upstream** — not an unresolved permission.

🔵 **And the finding that should change how this base reads grant files.** The same 1,865 bytes then do something no other repository on this shelf does: they **enumerate every component and its licence, and state what is deliberately not redistributed** — the project's own code and LoRA adapters (Apache-2.0), a vendored `TextComplexityToolkit` (MIT) with its path given, `TAALED` inside it, the Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct base model (Apache-2.0, *"obtained separately from Hugging Face; NOT redistributed"*), and the Persuade 2.0 corpus (*"original academic-use license … the raw corpus is NOT redistributed here"*). It closes with a bias notice warning against high-stakes automated decisions without human oversight.

That is precisely the dependency-graph discipline this page spent two passes demanding — **and it is in the repository with the technically defective grant, while the 42.7k★ repository with a perfect MIT payload quietly builds an LGPL-3.0 component.** The lesson is not that size tests are useless; it is that **a short grant file is a reason to read it, not a reason to reject it**, and that licence *hygiene* and licence *form* are independent variables. Five of the six classes would pass a licence-badge check; this one fails the badge check and passes on substance.

## 🆕 p676 — four rows placed, three refused, and the shelf's own promotion shortcut condemned

**Where these four came from.** Not from a search leg. The four mandated global legs and the four regional legs scored **`21/21` facts already held** against the live tree (`evidence/eight-leg-scoring.2026-11-06-p676.tsv`), controls clean at both signs — the **twenty-sixth** consecutive nil. Every row added this pass came from **`evidence/address-ledger.tsv`**, by taking addresses carried with `filed_on = -` and re-reading the grant payload at the real default ref. That is `p665`'s promotion channel, still the only one yielding, **twenty-three passes on**.

🔴 **But this pass found the shortcut inside that channel, and it fails unsafe.** The ledger has a `last_verdict` column. Promoting off it is the obvious economy — it is already measured, and it is how a backlog is *supposed* to be worked. **Of the `5` backlog addresses whose payloads were re-read against their ledger verdict, `2` contradicted it, and both contradictions point the wrong way:**

| Address | Ledger said | The payload says | Direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| [`leemonade/leemons`](https://github.com/leemonade/leemons) | **`MIT`** since `p12`, `7` passes | **Fair Code License**, built on the **Sustainable Use License** — *not OSI*, owner **Leemons Edtech Solutions SL**, Madrid | 🔴 **UNSAFE** — a commercial source-available grant recorded as permissive |
| [`beltromatti/get-it`](https://github.com/beltromatti/get-it) | **`Apache-2.0`** since `p123`, `9` passes | **modified Apache-2.0** — the §6 trademark clause is edited and `p555`'s grader returns **`SUBSTANTIVE`** | 🔴 **UNSAFE** — an altered grant recorded as the stock licence |
| [`opensalt/opensalt`](https://github.com/opensalt/opensalt) | `GPL-3.0｜Apache-2.0` since `p14` | **MIT**, verbatim, © *2016 Public Consulting Group* | 🟢 safe — but it **hid permissive supply for 6 passes** |

**So the rule this shelf now runs under: the ledger addresses a payload; it does not grade one.** Re-read the grant every time, at the real default ref. The two unsafe rows would have been filed here as MIT and Apache-2.0 — on this page, under the heading that promises *“the licences Globant can build commercial derivatives on.”*

🔴 **Found, read, and refused at p676 — with the evidence, so the next pass does not re-buy them:**

| Refused | Stars | Why not filed |
|---|---|---|

The two edits nearly cancel in length. `gh api` says `Apache-2.0`. **The operative terms were edited and three separate signals waved it through.** Built at **GDG AI Hack Milan 2026** — a real, 1,152★ education agent this base may not build on. The shelf is not short of addresses — this base's ledger carries **`1,497` unfiled addresses whose recorded verdict is permissive**. What it is short of is addresses that are permissive **when re-read**, on-domain, and **maintained**. Those three gates, applied to the `30` best-tracked backlog candidates, passed `15` and filed `4` here. The binding constraint is not supply and it is not the search index; it is **maintenance**.
