🔴 **Refused, and named so it is not re-proposed.** [`Jellypod-Inc/school-skills`](https://github.com/Jellypod-Inc/school-skills) — MIT (`1,065 B`, sha256₁₆ `d9290be59ddc`, `Copyright (c) 2026 Jellypod`), **11★**, self-described *"Alpha preview. Seven skills ship today; six more are on the way."* 🔴 **Unplaced:** the README names no jurisdiction, no standards body and no institution, and a company name alone is not an address. 🔵 It is the fifth instance of a category this shelf already holds (`anthropics/k12-teacher-skills`, `GarethManning/education-agent-skills`, `flysheep-ai/education-skills`, `virtualryder/edu-ai-agents`), so it is recorded in [`trending.md`](trending.md) rather than shelved here. **Fewer real rows beat padding.**

🔧 **Corrected, and it changes what this shelf's patterns may ship.** The `CC BY-NC-SA 4.0` clause this base has attached to the **PERSUADE** corpus since p57 was recorded under `P1007` — a principle that says to read the **author's** repository. 🔴 **Both** author repositories grant **CC BY 4.0** ([`persuade_corpus_2.0`](https://github.com/scrosseye/persuade_corpus_2.0), [`PERSUADE_corpus`](https://github.com/scrosseye/PERSUADE_corpus)). 🔵 The `NC-SA` sentence belongs to the **same author's other corpus**, CLEAR, in the same one-sentence form one token apart — and the redistributor repeats the substitution. 🟢 Net effect: **an essay-scoring engagement may train on upstream PERSUADE 2.0 commercially with attribution**; it may not use CLEAR, and it may not use Learning Commons' annotation layer on either. Measurement in [`trending.md`](trending.md) `675-A`; the two corpora are now shelved at [`../repos/foundations.md`](../repos/foundations.md).

🟢 **Headline rows re-read on the rendered channel and they hold:** `DeepTutor` **55.5k★** Apache-2.0, now at release **`v1.7.1` (2026.11.1)**; `OpenMAIC` **42.7k★** MIT; `educhain` **387★** MIT — ⚠️ whose README still calls **`v0.3.13` (October 2024)** the *"Current Version"*, a two-year-stale version ladder on a repository that pushed in May 2026; `OATutor` **276★** MIT.

## 🔴 Corrections to rows this KB previously published

These are not new findings — they are errors in data this knowledge base was already serving. Each was re-measured first-hand this pass.

| Row | Was published as | Measured 2026-11-04 | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|---|
| `kvnloo/tutor-mcp` | "MIT, **0 ★**, no adoption, no external review — watch it; do not start a client engagement on it" | **A fork.** The repository page states "forked from ArnaudGuiovanna/tutor-mcp". The upstream has **96 ★ / 13 forks**; the fork has 0 / 0. | The verdict was right about the address and wrong about the project. Both carry an identical 1,073 B MIT payload and an identical description, so every channel this KB measures agreed — only the fork notice separates them, and it lives on the page. The upstream is now filed above. `intel/trends.md` **T9** cited the 0-star figure as evidence that knowledge tracing is commercially immature; **that specific evidence is withdrawn** (the broader claim stands on pyKT 457★ and OATutor 276★). |
| `DaRL-GenAI/instructional_agents` | Apache-2.0 | **MIT** (payload 1,067 B, title `MIT License`) | Wrong grant named. Both are permissive, so the risk is low — but the cell was wrong. |
| `GeminiLight/awesome-ai-llm4education` | MIT, 1.5k ★ | **No licence file at all**, 221 ★ | A repo with no grant is *not* MIT. Building on it assumes a permission that was never given. Star count inflated 6.8×. |
| `mwasifanwar/eduadapt-ai` | MIT, ~150 ★ | **No licence file at all**, 26 ★ | Same defect: a false permissive grant. 26 stars is not an adopted project. Removed from this shelf. |
| `Open-TutorAi/open-tutor-ai-CE` | ~800 ★ | **116 ★** | Inflated 6.9×. Licence (BSD-3-Clause) was correct. |
| `HKUDS/DeepTutor` | 22k–24k ★ | **55,000 ★** | Understated by more than half. The row was stale, not wrong in kind. |

**The pattern:** two rows asserted a permissive licence that does not exist in the repository; four of five star counts were wrong in the same direction (inflated) except DeepTutor; and one row measured the wrong address entirely. Star counts on this shelf should be treated as measured-on-date, never carried forward.

🟢 **And the counter-result, from p651:** every one of the seven licence payloads above was re-read from bytes one pass after being measured, and all seven reproduced exactly; DeepTutor (55.0k ★) and OpenMAIC (42.7k ★) both re-measured to the published figure. The inflation was in values *carried forward from an unverified pipeline*, not in measurement itself. **Measured-and-dated holds; carried-forward does not.**

## 🟡 Found, real, and deliberately not filed

Recorded so a later pass does not re-spend the search — and so absence is not mistaken for a gap.

🔴 **p662 added no row to this shelf, and the reason is the measurement rather than a judgement.** The four supply-side legs returned **`67` facts, `64` already at HEAD**, and every in-scope repository they named was already filed: `THU-MAIC/OpenMAIC`, `HKUDS/DeepTutor`, `patchy631/ai-engineering-hub`, `learningequality/kolibri`, `sakaiproject/sakai`, `OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT`, Open edX, Moodle, `OpenEduCat`, `GibbonEdu/core`, `RosarioSIS`, FenixEdu. **`29` stands.**

🟡 **Three addresses the legs surfaced are held in the logs and the ledger, are MIT, and are still not filed — because none is education-sector software.** All three were verified first-hand at p662 anyway, so the decision rests on scope and not on doubt:

Decomposes automated essay scoring into three separable parts: a discourse-move classifier, a grade-independent LightGBM scorer over 31 linguistic and discourse features, and a label-aware feedback generator; LoRA adapters Apache-2.0, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct base obtained separately. **2 commits and 1 fork** — this is the artefact accompanying a publication. Not filed as an agent because there is nothing to adopt yet; recorded because it is the nearest thing to the gap, and because its grant file is instructive in both directions (see the defect note). ⚠ The Persuade 2.0 training corpus is academic-use-only and not redistributed, so a commercial retrain needs its own data. |
| [littlecookie0722/AI-Teaching-Agent](https://github.com/littlecookie0722/AI-Teaching-Agent) | MIT (1,069 B) | **0** | 🔴 **Shaped exactly like this base's assessment gap, and its author has frozen the two parts that would close it.** One Markdown source → a Lab and Exam package plus slides; generated artefacts enter a `WAITING_REVIEW` state requiring explicit human approve/reject; grading runs in Docker on a sandbox path; local-only export with no automatic publishing; an `mcp_server/` with a stdio server and tool adapters. 30 commits, **0 stars, 0 forks** — and the README states that *"Automatic grading productization"* and *"MCP/Agent expansion"* are **frozen**. So the two capabilities that matter are declared not under development. **Read it for the review-state machine** — `WAITING_REVIEW` plus sandboxed evaluation plus no-auto-publish is the right shape for a high-risk deployment under the EU AI Act — and build the rest. |
| [braiinz94/braiinz-education](https://github.com/braiinz94/braiinz-education) | **Apache-2.0 code (11,356 B) / CC BY-SA 4.0 content** | **0** | 🟡 **EMEA-placeable on documentary evidence, and split-licensed in the way that matters.** Socratic tutor that asks rather than answers, a diagnostician scheduling reviews with **FSRS**, and pluggable curricula including a **French *Seconde* mathematics pack** named in the README — which is the placement channel, a stated target curriculum rather than an inferred nationality. Telegram-native, no learner install. 37 commits, **0 stars / 1 fork**. Not filed on two counts: 0 stars is not adoption, and **the licence split puts the valuable part under ShareAlike** — the code is Apache-2.0 but the curriculum content is CC BY-SA 4.0, so a French K-12 engagement's most reusable asset cannot be folded into closed IP. The README is explicit that the project is separate from Braiinz, a commercial AI consultancy; no institution is named. |

It is a *different project* from `zijinz456/OpenTutor` (MIT, 166★), which is filed above. Neither is a fork of the other: same name, different owners, different licence status. **"OpenTutor" is therefore not a resolvable identifier in a proposal** — cite the owner. |

Usable as-is with attribution — not usable as a base for closed IP. It is also content and prompts, not software. |

## Agents by function

### Personalised tutoring
- **DeepTutor** — the default choice. Persistent learner model is the differentiator; a session chatbot is not a substitute.
- **Bloom** — lighter, pedagogy-led, and it ships as a Claude Code skill, which makes it the fastest thing here to demo.
- **OATutor** — structured BKT tutoring with LTI. Choose it when the client wants explainable mastery estimates rather than free-form dialogue, especially in maths.
- **Open TutorAI CE** — the only one with voice + avatar out of the box.
- **GenMentor** — goal-first rather than curriculum-first: the learner names an outcome and the agents work backwards to a session plan. Choose it when the client's learners are adults with a job-shaped goal, not students following a syllabus.
- 🆕 **Profe Bit** — the first **programming** tutor on this shelf, and the only row that executes learner code **in the browser** (Pyodide), so there is no server-side sandbox to operate or secure. Socratic by construction: the course is negotiated in conversation, then delivered as challenges rather than lessons. Spanish-language, Universidad Nacional de Colombia. `0★`/151 commits/314 tests — prior art, not a dependency.

### Classroom simulation
- **OpenMAIC** — unique on this shelf: it simulates *peers*, not just a tutor. AI classmates that disagree create discussion dynamics a one-to-one tutor cannot.

### Teaching-workload automation
- **Instructional Agents** — full ADDIE cycle to slides and assessments. The natural fit where a client has a shortage of instructional designers rather than a shortage of teachers.
- **AI-Shifu** — the authoring end rather than the delivery end: capture a subject expert's intent once, generate an adaptive course from it, embed it in the client's own portal. Apache-2.0, so the generated product can be commercial.

### Mastery and retention
- **tutor-mcp** — the only row here that packages Bayesian Knowledge Tracing *and* FSRS spaced repetition behind a single MCP tool surface. It is the bridge between the mastery layer (`pykt-toolkit`, `fsrs-rs` in `repos/foundations.md`) and any agent above. See `compose/patterns.md` **P6**.

### Local-first and sovereign deployment
- **OpenTutor** — the row to reach for when the binding constraint is *where the data goes*, not what the tutor can do. Ollama by default, no API key, nothing leaving the machine, FSRS 4.5 in-tree for scheduling, and Canvas import when the client does have a cloud LMS. 166★.
- **OmniEdu** — the model-layer upgrade underneath it, once the weight licence is resolved.
- 🆕 **Profe Bit** — second entry in this category and the only one that is local-first at **both** ends: the model through an OpenAI-compatible local server (Ollama), and code execution through Pyodide in the learner's own browser. Nothing in the loop requires an egress path, which is the test that matters where student data cannot leave the building.

### LMS integration
- **canvas-mcp** — the bridge layer for Canvas, which dominates North American higher education. Any of the tutors above reaches real enrolment, assignment and grade data through an MCP server like this one. 317★.
- **moodle-mcp** — the same job for Moodle, which dominates LATAM and much of EMEA. 27★. The asymmetry between those two numbers is the single most actionable fact on this page; see the gap note below.
- 🆕 **TutorIA** — the **Open edX** entry this section was missing. It ships as an XBlock/plugin rather than a service, so the tutor lands *inside* the platform's own course, enrolment and grading objects instead of talking to them over REST. That makes it the cheapest of the three bridges to deploy and the most opinionated: it is an agent **and** its integration, where `canvas-mcp` and `moodle-mcp` are integrations awaiting an agent. 🔵 **So the LMS-bridge picture now reads Canvas 317★ → Moodle 27★ → Open edX (in-platform, 0★) → OpenOlat 0**, and only the Open edX slot is filled by something that already contains the pedagogy.

### Model layer
- **OmniEdu** — the only row here that is not an agent. When a client needs the *model* to know the curriculum rather than the *prompt* to carry it — sovereign deployment, offline deployment, or K-12 subject grounding that a general model guesses at — this is the base. The 9B FP8 checkpoint is ~11 GiB, which puts curriculum-grounded tutoring on a single GPU. Gate on the weight licence first.

## 🔴 Open gaps on this page — stated so they are not re-discovered

- 🆕 🔄 **Re-derived at p672 from the `Region` column by counting it, and it supersedes every figure above: `48` rows — **Placed (28)**, **Unplaced (20)**.** **APAC (13)**, **North America (6)**, **LATAM (5)**, **EMEA (4)**. 🔴 **All five rows added at p672 are unplaced, and that is a measurement result rather than an omission.** Each owner was probed on `search/users`, which `667-G1` established is **three-valued** — `422` = login unsearchable, `0` = owner exists and no location match, `>=1` = placed. **All five returned `base=1` with `0` on every spelling probed** (`United States`/`USA`, `China`/`Beijing`, `Portugal`/`Lisbon`, `Bangladesh`, `Indonesia`, `India`), per `667-G2`'s rule that the field is free text and one spelling's `0` is not absence. So: **the owners exist and have published no location.** 🔵 **Two stronger channels were tried on these five and both came back empty, which is the useful part.** (1) *Named institution in the repository's own text* — `0` of `5`; (2) *organisational licensor in the grant payload* — `2` of `5` name one (`Towards AI, Inc.`, and `Cua AI, Inc.` on the foundations row), **and neither is a placement, because `Inc.` is a corporate form and not a location.** 🔴 **`672-C` — a named-institution detector must exclude licence context, or it places repositories in Massachusetts.** A `\bMIT\b` institution probe scored **`10` hits on `trycua/cua`'s README and `1`–`3` on three others**; every one was the *licence name*. The single most common institution-shaped token in open-source READMEs is a licence, so the two highest-value signals on this base's placement ladder — a named university and a permissive grant — **collide on one string**. Any institution scan this base runs must drop `MIT` unless it is adjacent to a word like `University`, `Institute`, `CSAIL` or `Media Lab`.

- 🟢 **The Moodle gap is restated, and it is a different kind of gap than this page has been recording.** Every prior pass declared that no permissively-licensed Moodle-side agent was found. p652 searched the category properly and found **seven, all MIT, in five languages** — and the entire field tops out at **27★**, against `canvas-mcp`'s **317★** for the same job on Canvas. So: **not a supply gap; a consolidation and adoption gap.** Six of the eight Moodle servers measured have ≤4 stars; none has external review at any meaningful scale. The consequence for an engagement is the opposite of what "no permissive agent exists" implied — the move is to adopt `G36maid/moodle-mcp` and harden it upstream, which is weeks of work, not to build from zero. The ~12× investment asymmetry against the LMS that dominates LATAM and EMEA, and in favour of the one that dominates North American HE, is itself a finding: open source has invested in Canvas-shaped institutions. Full measured field in `agents/trending.md` (p652); engagement shape in `compose/patterns.md` **P8**.
- 🟢 **Sixteen of forty-two rows are unplaced by region, and the shelf gained a region on a channel that outranks the one that moved it last pass. 🔄 Re-derived from the `Region` column at p669 by counting it, not by editing the previous figure.** **Placed (26):** **APAC (13)**, **LATAM (5)**, **EMEA (4)**, **North America (4)**. **Unplaced (16).** 🔧 **REPAIRED AT p669 — and this is the fourth instance of the defect this bullet itself warns about, found *inside* this bullet.** Until p669 the same sentence carried **two** sets of figures: p668's re-derived `Placed (24) / Unplaced (18)` **and**, a few clauses later, a stale p665-era `Placed (15) … Unplaced (22)` enumeration that no pass had re-derived. Both were presented as current. The stale half is deleted here rather than corrected, because a per-row placement list duplicates the `Region` column it is derived from and goes stale the moment the column moves — **which is exactly how it went stale.** The column is the single source; this bullet carries only counts taken from it. 🆕 **Two rows moved off `Global (unplaced)` at p669, on the *repository's own text*:** `vishalsachdev/canvas-mcp` → **North America** (README names University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign as the reference institution) and `HumphreySun98/Smart-Study-Agent` → **APAC** (Simplified-Chinese body text; region established, country not). 🔴 **A third candidate was refused, and the refusal is the more valuable half of the pass.** `RohanMuppa/brightspace-mcp-server` matched `TU Delft` → EMEA on the first arm. The match sits inside `(use --purdue at Purdue, --suny at SUNY, --tudelft at TU Delft, --cuny at CUNY, --leiden at Leiden, --mcgill at McGill, …)` — **six institutions, three countries, two regions.** The repository is **multi-tenant by construction**, which is a *finding about the artefact* and not a location. 🔵 **And the first guard built against it failed on its own control:** a guard that compares the regions its own signal dictionary recognises saw exactly **one** of those six institutions, because the other five are not in the dictionary — one match is no conflict, so it placed the row in EMEA with confidence. **A dictionary cannot police itself.** The working guard tests the *shape* of the line instead (≥3 CLI flags, ≥3 comma-separated proper nouns, ≥3 `at <Institution>` constructions) and needs no dictionary; it fires on this row and stays silent on all three true placements. 🔵 **The channels are ranked and each row says which it stands on.** Strongest: a **named institution in the repository's own text** (Profe Bit → Universidad Nacional de Colombia; `KI-Unterrichtswerkstatt` → *“berufsbildende Schule in Niedersachsen”*; 🆕 `canvas-mcp` → University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign). Then an **organisational licensor in the grant payload** (TutorIA → © *Grupo Sirius*). Then an **organisational owner account plus an unread DOI** (RUCAI → `RUC-Digital-Media-Lab`, Roskilde) and a **named national curriculum** (EduRAG → BNCC/PCN/PNLD), both marked provisional in-cell. Below those, **body-text language** (🆕 `Smart-Study-Agent`), admissible for the region field only. Weakest and flagged on all six rows that use it: the **owner account's self-declared `location` field** (p668). **Author names are still not a placement channel.** 🔴 **p668's instrument only works throttled, and that qualification carries forward:** `search/users` is capped at `30/min`; an unthrottled sweep exhausted the quota inside the first owner and reported **11 of its first 20 owners `UNSEARCHABLE`**, while the throttled rerun reported **`0` of `24`** with `user:moodle` returning `1` both before and after. **An exhausted quota is indistinguishable from an absent owner unless you read the response body.** 🔵 **The transferable mechanism is unchanged and now holds at four regions: a query-language gap in the instrument, not a supply gap in the sector.** LATAM closed at p663 off **Spanish** legs, EMEA at p664 and p665 off **German** legs, and North America moved at p665 for a different reason worth separating — `k12-teacher-skills` was in this repository's own ledger since `p17` with `27` measurements, so **that zero was a reading failure, not a language failure.** 🆕 **p669 adds a fifth kind: a placement that needed no new query at all**, only a read of a file the base had already fetched for its licence. ⚠️ **What the moved zeros do not mean.** EMEA's rows include a `0★` research artefact and a `1★` pilot; `canvas-mcp` is `317★` but its region cell describes **where it is deployed**, not a vendor presence. **Placed, permissive, documented prior art is not an adoptable production base, and that gap is the engagement** — see `compose/patterns.md` **P28**, **P29** and **P31**.
- 🆕 🔄 **Re-derived at p670 from the `Region` column, and it supersedes the figures in the bullet above: `43` rows — **Placed (28)**, **Unplaced (15)**.** **APAC (13)**, **North America (6)**, **LATAM (5)**, **EMEA (4)**. North America gained `2`: `DaRL-GenAI/instructional_agents` (placed at p670) and the new `instructional_agents_studio` row. 🔵 **Both stand on the grant-payload channel, and the second one widens it:** p669 ranked “organisational licensor in the grant payload” second only to a named institution in the repository's own text. p670 shows the payload channel **reaches across an owner account** — a repository whose own LICENSE names no institution can be placed by a sibling's, provided the owner is the same and nothing conflicts. 🔴 **Two placement channels were refuted at p670 and must not be re-tried:** (1) a **localized README** (`6/16` rows ship one; `0/16` have a non-English primary README; the counterexample is the ASU row's own `README.zh.md`) and (2) the **nationality of a consumer email provider** (one author, `yhygreat@qq.com` and `huaiyuan@asu.edu`). 🟡 **The commit-author channel is admissible but *sparse*, and p670 bounded it:** all `16` unplaced repositories were cloned at `--depth 200` and read; an institutional or national-TLD address appears in only `3`, and in all three as a **single drive-by commit** — `rosaunde@tcd.ie` **1/245** on `anki-mcp-server`, `atwong@usc.edu` **1/198** on `brightspace-mcp-server`, `lmgveerhoek` 2/198. `13` of `16` have a *dominant* author on a global consumer or pseudonymous address (`gmail`/`proton`/`qq`/`users.noreply`). **A drive-by is not a placement**, so none of the three moved. The channel paid out exactly once this pass — at `48/48` on `asu.edu` — which is the shape to look for: **dominance, not presence.** 🟢 **It also independently confirmed p669's refusal of `RohanMuppa/brightspace-mcp-server`:** the only institutional address in its 198-commit history is that `1`-commit USC drive-by, and the repository remains multi-tenant by construction. **Two channels, same refusal.**
- 🟢 **The licence-payload placement channel paid out again at p653, and this time on a platform.** `OpenOLAT/OpenOLAT`'s Apache-2.0 payload names *"the OLAT Zentrum at the University of Zurich"* and *"initiated by frentix GmbH"* inside the grant file itself — so the row is placed in **EMEA** on documentary evidence, with no inference from the author's name. That is the third successful use of this channel (after `ai-shifu` → APAC on README attribution and `thm-mni-ii/feedbacksystem` → EMEA on a copyright line), and it is the cheapest one available: it costs the same `curl` that reads the licence. A fourth, weaker variant appeared this pass: `braiinz94/braiinz-education` is placeable to **EMEA (France)** on a *stated target curriculum* — a named French *Seconde* mathematics pack — which is evidence about the market, not the origin, and is recorded as such.
- 🔴 **New gap, declared at p653 so it is not re-searched: there is no OpenOlat agent bridge of any kind.** `openolat-mcp`, `frentix/openolat-mcp` and `OpenOLAT/openolat-mcp` were probed and **none resolves**, and no MCP server for OpenOlat was found by search. So the LMS-bridge picture across the three platforms this base tracks now reads: **Canvas 317★ → Moodle 27★ → OpenOlat 0**. 🔵 **But this gap is a different kind from the Moodle one, and the difference is the opportunity.** Moodle's gap is a consolidation problem — seven MIT servers competing at ≤27★, so the work is hardening someone else's code and contributing upstream. OpenOlat has no incumbent at all, *and* it is the only one of the three whose platform licence (Apache-2.0) permits a closed derivative, *and* its LLM plumbing (`langchain4j`) is already in the tree, so an agent can live in-tree rather than talking over REST. That is the cleanest greenfield position on this shelf: no competitor to displace, no copyleft to route around, no bridge to write before the agent starts. See `compose/patterns.md` **P12**.
- 🟡 **The assessment gap, fourth pass: it has stopped being a supply gap and become an adoption gap — the same shape the Moodle gap turned out to have.** Earlier passes recorded no permissive assessment or grading agent at all. p651 found two education-specific ones with **no usable grant**. p652 searched the adjacent category and found one permissive general primitive, `microsoft/LLM-Rubric` (MIT, 45★), **not education-specific**. **p653 searched the automated-essay-scoring literature instead of the agent ecosystem, and the supply exists:** `wwrwbs/AI_AWE` / **ArguLens** (Apache-2.0 by reference, education-specific, scorer *and* label-aware feedback generator, arXiv:2608.17356) and `littlecookie0722/AI-Teaching-Agent` (MIT, exam generation plus a `WAITING_REVIEW` human-approval state machine and sandboxed grading). **Restated precisely: permissively-licensed, education-specific assessment software now demonstrably exists — at 2 stars / 2 commits and 0 stars / 30 commits respectively, with the latter's grading and MCP tracks declared *frozen* by its own author.** So the finding is no longer "nothing exists"; it is "everything that exists is a research artefact". That is a *better* position than three passes ago, because adopt-and-harden on a published, peer-reviewed decomposition is a scopeable engagement and building a grader from zero is not. This remains the one place on this shelf where Globant would be creating the production artefact rather than adopting one.
- 🔧 **CORRECTED AT p661 — this bullet said *"proctoring and assessment integrity remain genuinely unsupplied under a permissive licence … Nothing found in any pass, p653 included"*, and it was false when it was written.** `agents/trending.md:28089` has held [`lebmatter/exampro`](https://github.com/lebmatter/exampro) — **MIT, `72★`, live, proctored exams for the Frappe framework** — since **`p202`**, which is `451` passes before the sentence naming p653. Five further permissive integrity addresses sat in the same logs: [`vardanagarwal/Proctoring-AI`](https://github.com/vardanagarwal/Proctoring-AI) (MIT, `633★`, `p202`), [`AarambhDevHub/exam-cheating-detection`](https://github.com/AarambhDevHub/exam-cheating-detection) (MIT, `48★`), [`vincenzo-afk/Proctored-MCQ-Exam-Platform`](https://github.com/vincenzo-afk/Proctored-MCQ-Exam-Platform) (MIT, `37★`), [`Scaler-Opensource/exam-guard`](https://github.com/Scaler-Opensource/exam-guard) (Apache-2.0, `3★`, `p406`) and, as a counter-category, [`Kdmeenaa/CloakBox`](https://github.com/Kdmeenaa/CloakBox) (Apache-2.0, `21★`, `p480` — software for hiding a VM *from* proctoring tools). **All six re-verified first-hand at p661**: default branch via `git ls-remote --symref`, grant payload byte-read from `raw.githubusercontent.com`. Full table with bytes and `sha256₁₂` in `agents/trending.md` (`661-A`); the two platform-shaped rows are now on `verticals/solutions.md`. 🔵 **The corrected claim is not "the gap is closed" — it is the fourth instance of a shape this base keeps meeting: supply exists, and it is shallow and fragmented.** The highest-starred row is `633★` and has been **dormant for eleven months**; the live rows run `72★ / 48★ / 37★ / 3★`. The same shape as the Moodle bridges, assessment scoring and autograding. 🔴 **And here, uniquely on these shelves, shallow supply is a safety finding rather than an adoption note.** This remains the most regulated use case in education AI — EU AI Act Annex III high-risk, with emotion recognition in schools prohibited outright since February 2025. So **both** the old advice (*"no permissive supply, price a build"*) and the naive new reading (*"supply exists, adopt it"*) are wrong: a `3★` repository must not be carried into an Annex III deployment. **Treat this layer as prior art to read, not as a base to ship** — and note that `exampro`'s MIT grant reads `Copyright (c) [year] [fullname]`, an unfilled template that names no licensor at all (seventh grant-defect class, `repos/foundations.md`). 🟡 **The asymmetry the old bullet drew is withdrawn with it:** the *scoring* half and the *integrity* half both now have permissive supply, both at research or hobby scale, and the integrity half still carries all of the regulatory exposure.
- 🟢 **The model layer below the agents is open, and the gap in it is specific and gating.** `OmniEdu` is a K-12-grounded model family in three sizes with FP8 checkpoints 37–44% smaller than BF16 — the 9B at ~11 GiB, the 27B at ~28 GiB — which puts curriculum-grounded tutoring on single-GPU, in-jurisdiction, no-egress hardware. **The weights carry no named licence anywhere in the repository** and `huggingface.co` does not resolve under this session's network policy, so the cards remain unread at p653 as at p652. Nobody should deploy the weights on this page's authority. 🟢 **What p653 adds is that the no-egress path no longer depends on resolving that question**: `zijinz456/OpenTutor` defaults to Ollama with general open weights, and `OpenOlat`'s in-tree `langchain4j-open-ai` module will talk to any OpenAI-compatible endpoint, so a sovereign deployment can be demonstrated today with unambiguously-licensed weights and OmniEdu treated as a later upgrade.
